=== PROMPT VARIANT ===
r1

=== PROMPT ===
You will be given a competitive programming problem.
There are N cells numbered from 1 to N in a row. Initially, M cells contain stones, and cell X_i contains A_i stones (1 \leq i \leq M).
You can perform the following operation any number of times (possibly zero):

- If cell i (1 \leq i \leq N-1) contains a stone, move one stone from cell i to cell i+1.

Find the minimum number of operations required to reach a state where each of the N cells contains exactly one stone. If it is impossible, print -1.

Input

The input is given from Standard Input in the following format:
N M
X_1 X_2 \ldots X_M
A_1 A_2 \ldots A_M

Output

Print the answer.

Constraints


- 2 \leq N \leq 2 \times 10^{9}
- 1 \leq M \leq 2 \times 10^{5}
- M \leq N
- 1 \leq X_i \leq N (1 \leq i \leq M)
- X_i \neq X_j (1 \leq i < j \leq M)
- 1 \leq A_i \leq 2 \times 10^{9} (1 \leq i \leq M)
- All input values are integers.

Sample Input 1

5 2
1 4
3 2

Sample Output 1

4

You can reach a state where each of the five cells contains exactly one stone with four operations as follows:

- Move one stone from cell 1 to cell 2.
- Move one stone from cell 2 to cell 3.
- Move one stone from cell 4 to cell 5.
- Move one stone from cell 1 to cell 2.

It is impossible to achieve the goal in three or fewer operations. Therefore, print 4.

Sample Input 2

10 3
1 4 8
4 2 4

Sample Output 2

-1

No matter how you perform the operations, you cannot reach a state where all ten cells contain exactly one stone. Therefore, print -1.

Use the following starter code to write the final solution.
```python

```

Reason in a commit-implement-recheck way.

A productive rhythm for this task usually looks like this:
- Isolate the decisive constraints, the target complexity, and at most one or two approaches that are still truly plausible.
- If two candidates remain, compare them only on the decisive constraint and commit quickly to the stronger one instead of keeping a wide search open.
- Before coding, keep only a tiny implementation ledger for the chosen plan: main invariant, key state or transition, and the one or two edge cases most likely to fail.
- Move into code as soon as that ledger is clear. After coding starts, keep reasoning attached to the current draft rather than reopening the high-level search.
- After the first complete draft, do one short implementation-check-adjust-check loop on the same draft: test the key edge case, patch locally if needed, and recheck.
- Reopen the main design only if a concrete counterexample breaks the core invariant. Otherwise prefer the local patch and keep closing.
- Once the current draft, invariant, and short recheck still agree, finalize the solution.

Output only the final Python code enclosed in a code block.

Let's draft code:

import sys

def main():
    data = list(map(int, sys.stdin.buffer.read().split()))
    if not data:
        return
    N = data[0]
    M = data[1]
    X = data[2:2+M]
    A = data[2+M:2+2*M]

    total = sum(A)
    if total != N:
        print(-1)
        return

    initial_sum = sum(x * a for x, a in zip(X, A))
    pairs = sorted(zip(X, A))
    pref = 0
    for x, a in pairs:
        if pref < x - 1:
            print(-1)
            return
        pref += a

    # pref should be N
    final_sum = N * (N + 1) // 2
    print(final_sum - initial_sum)

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()

        print(-1); return
    pref += a

Now, let's consider if there is a possibility that total=N and prefix condition passes but there is a stone at position x_i and count a_i, and pref before x_i >= x_i-1, but after adding, pref maybe > N? total=N so no. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "M <= N" but if M=2e5 and N=2e9, total=N, A_i can be large. Sorting pairs by X; if X list not sorted, sorting. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possibility that total=N and prefix condition passes but there is a gap before first position x1=1? If x1=1, okay. If x1=1 and A1 maybe 0? no. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a hidden issue with "cell i contains a stone" if cell i has a stone but cell i+1 is outside for i=N; operation only up to N-1. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possibility that total=N and prefix condition passes but answer formula final_sum - initial_sum maybe not minimal because you could choose to not move some stones and instead move others farther, but final sum fixed; total distance sum fixed. The number of operations equals total distance. If you move a stone farther, another must move less? But final cells distinct and total sum fixed, so total distance fixed. There is no way to reduce below fixed total distance because each operation increments potential by 1. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "minimum number of operations" if impossible due to prefix condition but maybe you can move stones right to create empty cells and then fill them with stones from left, but if no stone from left, impossible. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a hidden "N cells numbered from 1 to N in a row" and "Initially, M cells contain stones" maybe there are stones only in M cells, but if A_i sum=N, then some cells have multiple stones and some empty. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possibility that total=N and prefix condition passes but there is a stone at position x_i and count a_i, and a_i is so large that it fills cells beyond N? We showed fail if too large. But what if there are earlier stones that can fill some cells after x_i, reducing the need for stones at x_i to fill beyond N? Actually if a_i is large, stones at x_i cannot move left, so they must occupy cells >=x_i. If there are more stones at x_i than cells from x_i to N, impossible regardless of earlier stones because earlier stones cannot occupy cells <x_i? Wait earlier stones can occupy cells <x_i or >=x_i. But cells from x_i to N are N-x_i+1 cells. If A_i > N-x_i+1, even if all those cells are filled by stones at x_i, there are extra stones at x_i with no destination. Earlier stones cannot reduce the number of stones at x_i; they could occupy some cells >=x_i, making even fewer available. So impossible. Prefix condition catches as shown. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possibility that total=N and prefix condition passes but there is a stone at position x_i with count a_i and a_i > N - x_i +1 but pref before x_i >= x_i-1? Is that possible? Let's compute pref before = N - a_i (since total=N and no positions after? Wait there may be positions after x_i, so pref before = N - a_i - sum_after. If a_i > N-x_i+1, then N - a_i - sum_after < x_i -1 - sum_after <= x_i-1. So pref before < x_i-1. Thus fail. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a hidden issue with "minimum operations" if answer formula final_sum - initial_sum maybe not minimal because you could move a stone from i to i+1 and then later another stone from i+1 to i+2, but the first stone at i+1 might be moved further? That would be extra operations and final sum would exceed? No, if first stone moved further, its final position changes, but final sum fixed; another stone would have to end earlier, but stones can't move left, so maybe impossible. Minimal is direct. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possibility that total=N and prefix condition passes but there is a stone at position p assigned to final q, and another stone at position p' assigned to final q' with p<p' but q>q' (crossing). Could crossing reduce operations? Since final sum fixed, total distance fixed? Wait total distance = sum final positions - sum initial positions, independent of assignment. So crossing doesn't affect total operations. But crossing might be infeasible if q' < p'? Sorted matching avoids crossing and is feasible if condition. But any feasible assignment yields same total distance. So no issue. Our existence proof via greedy gives noncrossing assignment. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possibility that total=N and prefix condition passes but answer formula final_sum - initial_sum maybe not minimal because final sum fixed but if stones are indistinguishable, maybe you don't need to assign each initial stone to a distinct final cell? But final has N stones in N cells, so yes each cell one stone, total N stones, so each initial stone corresponds to one final cell. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a hidden issue with "cell i contains a stone" if cell i has a stone but cell i+1 is occupied by a stone that is supposed to stay, moving into it would make two stones, but later maybe move one out. No restriction. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possibility that total=N and prefix condition passes but there is a stone at position p and assigned final q, and q-p operations. If multiple stones at same p, moving them one by one along same path; total operations sum. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a hidden "minimum number of operations" if you can perform operation any number of times, but if a cell contains multiple stones, moving one stone from i to i+1 might be considered moving "a stone" but if there are multiple, you can choose which. Indistinguishable. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possibility that total=N and prefix condition passes but there is a gap before first position x1=1? If x1=1, okay. If x1=1 and A1 maybe 0? no. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a hidden issue with "cell i contains a stone" if cell i has a stone but cell i+1 is outside for i=N; operation only up to N-1. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possibility that total=N and prefix condition passes but answer formula final_sum - initial_sum maybe not minimal because you could choose to not move some stones and instead move others farther, but final sum fixed; total distance sum fixed. The number of operations equals total distance. If you move a stone farther, another must move less? But final cells distinct and total sum fixed, so total distance fixed. There is no way to reduce below fixed total distance because each operation increments potential by 1. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "minimum number of operations" if impossible due to prefix condition but maybe you can move stones right to create empty cells and then fill them with stones from left, but if no stone from left, impossible. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a hidden "N cells numbered from 1 to N in a row" and "Initially, M cells contain stones" maybe there are stones only in M cells, but if A_i sum=N, then some cells have multiple stones and some empty. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possibility that total=N and prefix condition passes but there is a stone at position x_i and count a_i, and a_i is so large that it fills cells beyond N? We showed fail if too large. But what if there are earlier stones that can fill some cells after x_i, reducing the need for stones at x_i to fill beyond N? Actually if a_i is large, stones at x_i cannot move left, so they must occupy cells >=x_i. If there are more stones at x_i than cells from x_i to N, impossible regardless of earlier stones because earlier stones cannot occupy cells <x_i? Wait earlier stones can occupy cells <x_i or >=x_i. But cells from x_i to N are N-x_i+1 cells. If A_i > N-x_i+1, even if all those cells are filled by stones at x_i, there are extra stones at x_i with no destination. Earlier stones cannot reduce the number of stones at x_i; they could occupy some cells >=x_i, making even fewer available. So impossible. Prefix condition catches as shown. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possibility that total=N and prefix condition passes but there is a stone at position x_i with count a_i and a_i > N - x_i +1 but pref before x_i >= x_i-1? Is that possible? Let's compute pref before = N - a_i - sum_after. If a_i > N-x_i+1, then N - a_i - sum_after < x_i -1 - sum_after <= x_i-1. So pref before < x_i-1. Thus fail. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a hidden issue with "minimum operations" if answer formula final_sum - initial_sum maybe not minimal because you could move a stone from i to i+1 and then later another stone from i+1 to i+2, but the first stone at i+1 might be moved further? That would be extra operations and final sum would exceed? No, if first stone moved further, its final position changes, but final sum fixed; another stone would have to end earlier, but stones can't move left, so maybe impossible. Minimal is direct. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possibility that total=N and prefix condition passes but there is a stone at position p assigned to final q, and another stone at position p' assigned to final q' with p<p' but q>q' (crossing). Could crossing reduce operations? Since final sum fixed, total distance = sum final positions - sum initial positions, independent of assignment. So crossing doesn't affect total operations. But crossing might be infeasible if q' < p'? Sorted matching avoids crossing and is feasible if condition. But any feasible assignment yields same total distance. So no issue. Our existence proof via greedy gives noncrossing assignment. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possibility that total=N and prefix condition passes but answer formula final_sum - initial_sum maybe not minimal because final sum fixed but if stones are indistinguishable, maybe you don't need to assign each initial stone to a distinct final cell? But final has N stones in N cells, so yes each cell one stone, total N stones, so each initial stone corresponds to one final cell. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a hidden issue with "cell i contains a stone" if cell i has a stone but cell i+1 is occupied by a stone that is supposed to stay, moving into it would make two stones, but later maybe move one out. No restriction. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possibility that total=N and prefix condition passes but there is a stone at position p and assigned final q, and q-p operations. If multiple stones at same p, moving them one by one along same path; total operations sum. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a hidden "minimum number of operations" if you can perform operation any number of times, but if a cell contains multiple stones, moving one stone from i to i+1 might be considered moving "a stone" but if there are multiple, you can choose which. Indistinguishable. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possibility that total=N and prefix condition passes but answer formula final_sum - initial_sum could be computed as `N * (N + 1) // 2 - initial_sum` and if initial_sum is large, Python int. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a need to handle `M` maybe not equal to len(X) if input malformed; no.

Now, let's think about if there is a hidden issue with "N M" first line, but using data[0], data[1] works. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possibility that total=N and prefix condition passes but answer formula final_sum - initial_sum maybe not minimal because you could choose to not move some stones and instead move others farther, but final sum fixed; total distance sum fixed. The number of operations equals total distance. If you move a stone farther, another must move less? But final cells distinct and total sum fixed, so total distance fixed. There is no way to reduce below fixed total distance because each operation increments potential by 1. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "minimum number of operations" if impossible due to prefix condition but maybe you can move stones right to create empty cells and then fill them with stones from left, but if no stone from left, impossible. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a hidden "N cells numbered from 1 to N in a row" and "Initially, M cells contain stones" maybe there are stones only in M cells, but if A_i sum=N, then some cells have multiple stones and some empty. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possibility that total=N and prefix condition passes but there is a stone at position x_i and count a_i, and a_i is so large that it fills cells beyond N? We showed fail if too large. But what if there are earlier stones that can fill some cells after x_i, reducing the need for stones at x_i to fill beyond N? Actually if a_i is large, stones at x_i cannot move left, so they must occupy cells >=x_i. If there are more stones at x_i than cells from x_i to N, impossible regardless of earlier stones because earlier stones cannot occupy cells <x_i? Wait earlier stones can occupy cells <x_i or >=x_i. But cells from x_i to N are N-x_i+1 cells. If A_i > N-x_i+1, even if all those cells are filled by stones at x_i, there are extra stones at x_i with no destination. Earlier stones cannot reduce the number of stones at x_i; they could occupy some cells >=x_i, making even fewer available. So impossible. Prefix condition catches as shown. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possibility that total=N and prefix condition passes but there is a stone at position x_i with count a_i and a_i > N - x_i +1 but pref before x_i >= x_i-1? Is that possible? Let's compute pref before = N - a_i - sum_after. If a_i > N-x_i+1, then N - a_i - sum_after < x_i -1 - sum_after <= x_i-1. So pref before < x_i-1. Thus fail. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a hidden issue with "minimum operations" if answer formula final_sum - initial_sum maybe not minimal because you could move a stone from i to i+1 and then later another stone from i+1 to i+2, but the first stone at i+1 might be moved further? That would be extra operations and final sum would exceed? No, if first stone moved further, its final position changes, but final sum fixed; another stone would have to end earlier, but stones can't move left, so maybe impossible. Minimal is direct. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possibility that total=N and prefix condition passes but there is a stone at position p assigned to final q, and another stone at position p' assigned to final q' with p<p' but q>q' (crossing). Could crossing reduce operations? Since final sum fixed, total distance = sum final positions - sum initial positions, independent of assignment. So crossing doesn't affect total operations. But crossing might be infeasible if q' < p'? Sorted matching avoids crossing and is feasible if condition. But any feasible assignment yields same total distance. So no issue. Our existence proof via greedy gives noncrossing assignment. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possibility that total=N and prefix condition passes but answer formula final_sum - initial_sum maybe not minimal because final sum fixed but if stones are indistinguishable, maybe you don't need to assign each initial stone to a distinct final cell? But final has N stones in N cells, so yes each cell one stone, total N stones, so each initial stone corresponds to one final cell. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a hidden issue with "cell i contains a stone" if cell i has a stone but cell i+1 is occupied by a stone that is supposed to stay, moving into it would make two stones, but later maybe move one out. No restriction. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possibility that total=N and prefix condition passes but there is a stone at position p and assigned final q, and q-p operations. If multiple stones at same p, moving them one by one along same path; total operations sum. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a hidden "minimum number of operations" if you can perform operation any number of times, but if a cell contains multiple stones, moving one stone from i to i+1 might be considered moving "a stone" but if there are multiple, you can choose which. Indistinguishable. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possibility that total=N and prefix condition passes but answer formula final_sum - initial_sum could be computed as `N * (N + 1) // 2 - initial_sum` and if initial_sum is large, Python int. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a need to handle `M` maybe not equal to len(X) if input malformed; no.

Now, let's think about if there is a hidden issue with "N M" first line, but using data[0], data[1] works. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possibility that total=N and prefix condition passes but there is a stone at position x_i and count a_i, and pref before x_i >= x_i-1, but after adding, pref maybe > N? total=N so no. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "M <= N" but if M=2e5 and N=2e9, total=N, A_i can be large. Sorting pairs by X; if X list not sorted, sorting. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a hidden "N cells numbered from 1 to N in a row" and "Initially, M cells contain stones" maybe there are stones only in M cells, but if A_i sum=N, then some cells have multiple stones and some empty. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possibility that total=N and prefix condition passes but there is a stone at position x_i and count a_i, and a_i is so large that it fills cells beyond N? We showed fail if too large. But what if there are earlier stones that can fill some cells after x_i, reducing the need for stones at x_i to fill beyond N? Actually if a_i is large, stones at x_i cannot move left, so they must occupy cells >=x_i. If there are more stones at x_i than cells from x_i to N, impossible regardless of earlier stones because earlier stones cannot occupy cells <x_i? Wait earlier stones can occupy cells <x_i or >=x_i. But cells from x_i to N are N-x_i+1 cells. If A_i > N-x_i+1, even if all those cells are filled by stones at x_i, there are extra stones at x_i with no destination. Earlier stones cannot reduce the number of stones at x_i; they could occupy some cells >=x_i, making even fewer available. So impossible. Prefix condition catches as shown. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possibility that total=N and prefix condition passes but there is a stone at position x_i with count a_i and a_i > N - x_i +1 but pref before x_i >= x_i-1? Is that possible? Let's compute pref before = N - a_i - sum_after. If a_i > N-x_i+1, then N - a_i - sum_after < x_i -1 - sum_after <= x_i-1. So pref before < x_i-1. Thus fail. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a hidden issue with "minimum operations" if answer formula final_sum - initial_sum maybe not minimal because you could move a stone from i to i+1 and then later another stone from i+1 to i+2, but the first stone at i+1 might be moved further? That would be extra operations and final sum would exceed? No, if first stone moved further, its final position changes, but final sum fixed; another stone would have to end earlier, but stones can't move left, so maybe impossible. Minimal is direct. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possibility that total=N and prefix condition passes but there is a stone at position p assigned to final q, and another stone at position p' assigned to final q' with p<p' but q>q' (crossing). Could crossing reduce operations? Since final sum fixed, total distance = sum final positions - sum initial positions, independent of assignment. So crossing doesn't affect total operations. But crossing might be infeasible if q' < p'? Sorted matching avoids crossing and is feasible if condition. But any feasible assignment yields same total distance. So no issue. Our existence proof via greedy gives noncrossing assignment. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possibility that total=N and prefix condition passes but answer formula final_sum - initial_sum maybe not minimal because final sum fixed but if stones are indistinguishable, maybe you don't need to assign each initial stone to a distinct final cell? But final has N stones in N cells, so yes each cell one stone, total N stones, so each initial stone corresponds to one final cell. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a hidden issue with "cell i contains a stone" if cell i has a stone but cell i+1 is occupied by a stone that is supposed to stay, moving into it would make two stones, but later maybe move one out. No restriction. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possibility that total=N and prefix condition passes but there is a stone at position p and assigned final q, and q-p operations. If multiple stones at same p, moving them one by one along same path; total operations sum. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a hidden "minimum number of operations" if you can perform operation any number of times, but if a cell contains multiple stones, moving one stone from i to i+1 might be considered moving "a stone" but if there are multiple, you can choose which. Indistinguishable. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possibility that total=N and prefix condition passes but answer formula final_sum - initial_sum could be computed as `N * (N + 1) // 2 - initial_sum` and if initial_sum is large, Python int. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a need to handle `M` maybe not equal to len(X) if input malformed; no.

Now, let's think about if there is a hidden issue with "N M" first line, but using data[0], data[1] works. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possibility that total=N and prefix condition passes but there is a stone at position x_i and count a_i, and pref before x_i >= x_i-1, but after adding, pref maybe > N? total=N so no. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "M <= N" but if M=2e5 and N=2e9, total=N, A_i can be large. Sorting pairs by X; if X list not sorted, sorting. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possibility that total=N and prefix condition passes but there is a gap before first position x1=1? If x1=1, okay. If x1=1 and A1 maybe 0? no. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a hidden issue with "cell i contains a stone" if cell i has a stone but cell i+1 is outside for i=N; operation only up to N-1. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possibility that total=N and prefix condition passes but answer formula final_sum - initial_sum maybe not minimal because you could choose to not move some stones and instead move others farther, but final sum fixed; total distance sum fixed. The number of operations equals total distance. If you move a stone farther, another must move less? But final cells distinct and total sum fixed, so total distance fixed. There is no way to reduce below fixed total distance because each operation increments potential by 1. Good.

If there are more stones at x_i than cells from x_i to N, impossible regardless of earlier stones because earlier stones cannot occupy cells <x_i? Wait earlier stones can occupy cells <x_i or >=x_i. But cells from x_i to N are N-x_i+1 cells. If A_i > N-x_i+1, even if all those cells are filled by stones at x_i, there are extra stones at x_i with no destination. Earlier stones cannot reduce the number of stones at x_i; they could occupy some cells >=x_i, making even fewer available. So impossible. Prefix condition catches as shown. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possibility that total=N and prefix condition passes but there is a stone at position x_i with count a_i and a_i > N - x_i +1 but pref before x_i >= x_i-1? Is that possible? Let's compute pref before = N - a_i - sum_after. If a_i > N-x_i+1, then N - a_i - sum_after < x_i -1 - sum_after <= x_i-1. So pref before < x_i-1. Thus fail. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a hidden issue with "minimum operations" if answer formula final_sum - initial_sum maybe not minimal because you could move a stone from i to i+1 and then later another stone from i+1 to i+2, but the first stone at i+1 might be moved further? That would be extra operations and final sum would exceed? No, if first stone moved further, its final position changes, but final sum fixed; another stone would have to end earlier, but stones can't move left, so maybe impossible. Minimal is direct. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possibility that total=N and prefix condition passes but there is a stone at position p assigned to final q, and another stone at position p' assigned to final q' with p<p' but q>q' (crossing). Could crossing reduce operations? Since final sum fixed, total distance = sum final positions - sum initial positions, independent of assignment. So crossing doesn't affect total operations. But crossing might be infeasible if q' < p'? Sorted matching avoids crossing and is feasible if condition. But any feasible assignment yields same total distance. So no issue. Our existence proof via greedy gives noncrossing assignment. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possibility that total=N and prefix condition passes but answer formula final_sum - initial_sum maybe not minimal because final sum fixed but if stones are indistinguishable, maybe you don't need to assign each initial stone to a distinct final cell? But final has N stones in N cells, so yes each cell one stone, total N stones, so each initial stone corresponds to one final cell. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a hidden issue with "cell i contains a stone" if cell i has a stone but cell i+1 is occupied by a stone that is supposed to stay, moving into it would make two stones, but later maybe move one out. No restriction. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possibility that total=N and prefix condition passes but there is a stone at position p and assigned final q, and q-p operations. If multiple stones at same p, moving them one by one along same path; total operations sum. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a hidden "minimum number of operations" if you can perform operation any number of times, but if a cell contains multiple stones, moving one stone from i to i+1 might be considered moving "a stone" but if there are multiple, you can choose which. Indistinguishable. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possibility that total=N and prefix condition passes but answer formula final_sum - initial_sum could be computed as `N * (N + 1) // 2 - initial_sum` and if initial_sum is large, Python int. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a need to handle `M` maybe not equal to len(X) if input malformed; no.

Now, let's think about if there is a hidden issue with "N M" first line, but using data[0], data[1] works. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possibility that total=N and prefix condition passes but there is a stone at position x_i and count a_i, and pref before x_i >= x_i-1, but after adding, pref maybe > N? total=N so no. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "M <= N" but if M=2e5 and N=2e9, total=N, A_i can be large. Sorting pairs by X; if X list not sorted, sorting. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possibility that total=N and prefix condition passes but there is a gap before first position x1=1? If x1=1, okay. If x1=1 and A1 maybe 0? no. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a hidden issue with "cell i contains a stone" if cell i has a stone but cell i+1 is outside for i=N; operation only up to N-1. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possibility that total=N and prefix condition passes but answer formula final_sum - initial_sum maybe not minimal because you could choose to not move some stones and instead move others farther, but final sum fixed; total distance sum fixed. The number of operations equals total distance. If you move a stone farther, another must move less? But final cells distinct and total sum fixed, so total distance fixed. There is no way to reduce below fixed total distance because each operation increments potential by 1. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "minimum number of operations" if impossible due to prefix condition but maybe you can move stones right to create empty cells and then fill them with stones from left, but if no stone from left, impossible. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a hidden "N cells numbered from 1 to N in a row" and "Initially, M cells contain stones" maybe there are stones only in M cells, but if A_i sum=N, then some cells have multiple stones and some empty. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possibility that total=N and prefix condition passes but there is a stone at position x_i with count a_i and a_i > N - x_i +1 but pref before x_i >= x_i-1? Is that possible? Let's compute pref before = N - a_i - sum_after. If a_i > N-x_i+1, then N - a_i - sum_after < x_i -1 - sum_after <= x_i-1. So pref before < x_i-1. Thus fail. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a hidden issue with "minimum operations" if answer formula final_sum - initial_sum maybe not minimal because you could move a stone from i to i+1 and then later another stone from i+1 to i+2, but the first stone at i+1 might be moved further? That would be extra operations and final sum would exceed? No, if first stone moved further, its final position changes, but final sum fixed; another stone would have to end earlier, but stones can't move left, so maybe impossible. Minimal is direct. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possibility that total=N and prefix condition passes but there is a stone at position p assigned to final q, and another stone at position p' assigned to final q' with p<p' but q>q' (crossing). Could crossing reduce operations? Since final sum fixed, total distance = sum final positions - sum initial positions, independent of assignment. So crossing doesn't affect total operations. But crossing might be infeasible if q' < p'? Sorted matching avoids crossing and is feasible if condition. But any feasible assignment yields same total distance. So no issue. Our existence proof via greedy gives noncrossing assignment. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possibility that total=N and prefix condition passes but answer formula final_sum - initial_sum maybe not minimal because final sum fixed but if stones are indistinguishable, maybe you don't need to assign each initial stone to a distinct final cell? But final has N stones in N cells, so yes each cell one stone, total N stones, so each initial stone corresponds to one final cell. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a hidden issue with "cell i contains a stone" if cell i has a stone but cell i+1 is occupied by a stone that is supposed to stay, moving into it would make two stones, but later maybe move one out. No restriction. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possibility that total=N and prefix condition passes but there is a stone at position p and assigned final q, and q-p operations. If multiple stones at same p, moving them one by one along same path; total operations sum. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a hidden "minimum number of operations" if you can perform operation any number of times, but if a cell contains multiple stones, moving one stone from i to i+1 might be considered moving "a stone" but if there are multiple, you can choose which. Indistinguishable. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possibility that total=N and prefix condition passes but answer formula final_sum - initial_sum could be computed as `N * (N + 1) // 2 - initial_sum` and if initial_sum is large, Python int. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a need to handle `M` maybe not equal to len(X) if input malformed; no.

Now, let's think about if there is a hidden issue with "N M" first line, but using data[0], data[1] works. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possibility that total=N and prefix condition passes but there is a stone at position x_i and count a_i, and pref before x_i >= x_i-1, but after adding, pref maybe > N? total=N so no. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "M <= N" but if M=2e5 and N=2e9, total=N, A_i can be large. Sorting pairs by X; if X list not sorted, sorting. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possibility that total=N and prefix condition passes but there is a gap before first position x1=1? If x1=1, okay. If x1=1 and A1 maybe 0? no. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a hidden issue with "cell i contains a stone" if cell i has a stone but cell i+1 is outside for i=N; operation only up to N-1. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possibility that total=N and prefix condition passes but answer formula final_sum - initial_sum maybe not minimal because you could choose to not move some stones and instead move others farther, but final sum fixed; total distance sum fixed. The number of operations equals total distance. If you move a stone farther, another must move less? But final cells distinct and total sum fixed, so total distance fixed. There is no way to reduce below fixed total distance because each operation increments potential by 1. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "minimum number of operations" if impossible due to prefix condition but maybe you can move stones right to create empty cells and then fill them with stones from left, but if no stone from left, impossible. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a hidden "N cells numbered from 1 to N in a row" and "Initially, M cells contain stones" maybe there are stones only in M cells, but if A_i sum=N, then some cells have multiple stones and some empty. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possibility that total=N and prefix condition passes but there is a stone at position x_i and count a_i, and a_i is so large that it fills cells beyond N? We showed fail if too large. But what if there are earlier stones that can fill some cells after x_i, reducing the need for stones at x_i to fill beyond N? Actually if a_i is large, stones at x_i cannot move left, so they must occupy cells >=x_i. If there are more stones at x_i than cells from x_i to N, impossible regardless of earlier stones because earlier stones cannot occupy cells <x_i? Wait earlier stones can occupy cells <x_i or >=x_i. But cells from x_i to N are N-x_i+1 cells. If A_i > N-x_i+1, even if all those cells are filled by stones at x_i, there are extra stones at x_i with no destination. Now, let's think about if there is a hidden issue with "minimum operations" if answer formula final_sum - initial_sum maybe not minimal because you could move a stone from i to i+1 and then later another stone from i+1 to i+2, but the first stone at i+1 might be moved further? That would be extra operations and final sum would exceed? No, if first stone moved further, its final position changes, but final sum fixed; another stone would have to end earlier, but stones can't move left, so maybe impossible. Minimal is direct. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possibility that total=N and prefix condition passes but there is a stone at position p assigned to final q, and another stone at position p' assigned to final q' with p<p' but q>q' (crossing). Could crossing reduce operations? Since final sum fixed, total distance = sum final positions - sum initial positions, independent of assignment. So crossing doesn't affect total operations. But crossing might be infeasible if q' < p'? Sorted matching avoids crossing and is feasible if condition. But any feasible assignment yields same total distance. So no issue. Our existence proof via greedy gives noncrossing assignment. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possibility that total=N and prefix condition passes but answer formula final_sum - initial_sum maybe not minimal because final sum fixed but if stones are indistinguishable, maybe you don't need to assign each initial stone to a distinct final cell? But final has N stones in N cells, so yes each cell one stone, total N stones, so each initial stone corresponds to one final cell. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a hidden issue with "cell i contains a stone" if cell i has a stone but cell i+1 is occupied by a stone that is supposed to stay, moving into it would make two stones, but later maybe move one out. No restriction. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possibility that total=N and prefix condition passes but there is a stone at position p and assigned final q, and q-p operations. If multiple stones at same p, moving them one by one along same path; total operations sum. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a hidden "minimum number of operations" if you can perform operation any number of times, but if a cell contains multiple stones, moving one stone from i to i+1 might be considered moving "a stone" but if there are multiple, you can choose which. Indistinguishable. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possibility that total=N and prefix condition passes but answer formula final_sum - initial_sum could be computed as `N * (N + 1) // 2 - initial_sum` and if initial_sum is large, Python int. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a need to handle `M` maybe not equal to len(X) if input malformed; no.

Now, let's think about if there is a hidden issue with "N M" first line, but using data[0], data[1] works. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possibility that total=N and prefix condition passes but there is a stone at position x_i and count a_i, and pref before x_i >= x_i-1, but after adding, pref maybe > N? total=N so no. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "M <= N" but if M=2e5 and N=2e9, total=N, A_i can be large. Sorting pairs by X; if X list not sorted, sorting. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possibility that total=N and prefix condition passes but there is a gap before first position x1=1? If x1=1, okay. If x1=1 and A1 maybe 0? no. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a hidden issue with "cell i contains a stone" if cell i has a stone but cell i+1 is outside for i=N; operation only up to N-1. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possibility that total=N and prefix condition passes but answer formula final_sum - initial_sum maybe not minimal because you could choose to not move some stones and instead move others farther, but final sum fixed; total distance sum fixed. The number of operations equals total distance. If you move a stone farther, another must move less? But final cells distinct and total sum fixed, so total distance fixed. There is no way to reduce below fixed total distance because each operation increments potential by 1. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "minimum number of operations" if impossible due to prefix condition but maybe you can move stones right to create empty cells and then fill them with stones from left, but if no stone from left, impossible. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a hidden "N cells numbered from 1 to N in a row" and "Initially, M cells contain stones" maybe there are stones only in M cells, but if A_i sum=N, then some cells have multiple stones and some empty. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possibility that total=N and prefix condition passes but there is a stone at position x_i and count a_i, and a_i is so large that it fills cells beyond N? We showed fail if too large. But what if there are earlier stones that can fill some cells after x_i, reducing the need for stones at x_i to fill beyond N? Actually if a_i is large, stones at x_i cannot move left, so they must occupy cells >=x_i. If there are more stones at x_i than cells from x_i to N, impossible regardless of earlier stones because earlier stones cannot occupy cells <x_i? Wait earlier stones can occupy cells <x_i or >=x_i. But cells from x_i to N are N-x_i+1 cells. If A_i > N-x_i+1, even if all those cells are filled by stones at x_i, there are extra stones at x_i with no destination. Earlier stones cannot reduce the number of stones at x_i; they could occupy some cells >=x_i, making even fewer available. So impossible. Prefix condition catches as shown. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possibility that total=N and prefix condition passes but there is a stone at position x_i with count a_i and a_i > N - x_i +1 but pref before x_i >= x_i-1? Now, let's consider if there is a possibility that total=N and prefix condition passes but there is a stone at position p assigned to final q, and another stone at position p' assigned to final q' with p<p' but q>q' (crossing). Could crossing reduce operations? Since final sum fixed, total distance = sum final positions - sum initial positions, independent of assignment. So crossing doesn't affect total operations. But crossing might be infeasible if q' < p'? Sorted matching avoids crossing and is feasible if condition. But any feasible assignment yields same total distance. So no issue. Our existence proof via greedy gives noncrossing assignment. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possibility that total=N and prefix condition passes but answer formula final_sum - initial_sum maybe not minimal because final sum fixed but if stones are indistinguishable, maybe you don't need to assign each initial stone to a distinct final cell? But final has N stones in N cells, so yes each cell one stone, total N stones, so each initial stone corresponds to one final cell. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a hidden issue with "cell i contains a stone" if cell i has a stone but cell i+1 is occupied by a stone that is supposed to stay, moving into it would make two stones, but later maybe move one out. No restriction. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possibility that total=N and prefix condition passes but there is a stone at position p and assigned final q, and q-p operations. If multiple stones at same p, moving them one by one along same path; total operations sum. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a hidden "minimum number of operations" if you can perform operation any number of times, but if a cell contains multiple stones, moving one stone from i to i+1 might be considered moving "a stone" but if there are multiple, you can choose which. Indistinguishable. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possibility that total=N and prefix condition passes but answer formula final_sum - initial_sum could be computed as `N * (N + 1) // 2 - initial_sum` and if initial_sum is large, Python int. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a need to handle `M` maybe not equal to len(X) if input malformed; no.

Now, let's think about if there is a hidden issue with "N M" first line, but using data[0], data[1] works. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possibility that total=N and prefix condition passes but there is a stone at position x_i and count a_i, and pref before x_i >= x_i-1, but after adding, pref maybe > N? total=N so no. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "M <= N" but if M=2e5 and N=2e9, total=N, A_i can be large. Sorting pairs by X; if X list not sorted, sorting. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possibility that total=N and prefix condition passes but there is a gap before first position x1=1? If x1=1, okay. If x1=1 and A1 maybe 0? no. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a hidden issue with "cell i contains a stone" if cell i has a stone but cell i+1 is outside for i=N; operation only up to N-1. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possibility that total=N and prefix condition passes but answer formula final_sum - initial_sum maybe not minimal because you could choose to not move some stones and instead move others farther, but final sum fixed; total distance sum fixed. The number of operations equals total distance. If you move a stone farther, another must move less? But final cells distinct and total sum fixed, so total distance fixed. There is no way to reduce below fixed total distance because each operation increments potential by 1. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "minimum number of operations" if impossible due to prefix condition but maybe you can move stones right to create empty cells and then fill them with stones from left, but if no stone from left, impossible. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a hidden "N cells numbered from 1 to N in a row" and "Initially, M cells contain stones" maybe there are stones only in M cells, but if A_i sum=N, then some cells have multiple stones and some empty. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possibility that total=N and prefix condition passes but there is a stone at position x_i and count a_i, and a_i is so large that it fills cells beyond N? We showed fail if too large. But what if there are earlier stones that can fill some cells after x_i, reducing the need for stones at x_i to fill beyond N? Actually if a_i is large, stones at x_i cannot move left, so they must occupy cells >=x_i. If there are more stones at x_i than cells from x_i to N, impossible regardless of earlier stones because earlier stones cannot occupy cells <x_i? Wait earlier stones can occupy cells <x_i or >=x_i. But cells from x_i to N are N-x_i+1 cells. If A_i > N-x_i+1, even if all those cells are filled by stones at x_i, there are extra stones at x_i with no destination. Earlier stones cannot reduce the number of stones at x_i; they could occupy some cells >=x_i, making even fewer available. So impossible. Prefix condition catches as shown. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possibility that total=N and prefix condition passes but there is a stone at position x_i with count a_i and a_i > N - x_i +1 but pref before x_i >= x_i-1? Is that possible? Let's compute pref before = N - a_i - sum_after. If a_i > N-x_i+1, then N - a_i - sum_after < x_i -1 - sum_after <= x_i-1. So pref before < x_i-1. Thus fail. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a hidden issue with "minimum operations" if answer formula final_sum - initial_sum maybe not minimal because you could move a stone from i to i+1 and then later another stone from i+1 to i+2, but the first stone at i+1 might be moved further? That would be extra operations and final sum would exceed? No, if first stone moved further, its final position changes, but final sum fixed; another stone would have to end earlier, but stones can't move left, so maybe impossible. Minimal is direct. Good.

But final has N stones in N cells, so yes each cell one stone, total N stones, so each initial stone corresponds to one final cell. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a hidden issue with "cell i contains a stone" if cell i has a stone but cell i+1 is occupied by a stone that is supposed to stay, moving into it would make two stones, but later maybe move one out. No restriction. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possibility that total=N and prefix condition passes but there is a stone at position p and assigned final q, and q-p operations. If multiple stones at same p, moving them one by one along same path; total operations sum. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a hidden "minimum number of operations" if you can perform operation any number of times, but if a cell contains multiple stones, moving one stone from i to i+1 might be considered moving "a stone" but if there are multiple, you can choose which. Indistinguishable. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possibility that total=N and prefix condition passes but answer formula final_sum - initial_sum could be computed as `N * (N + 1) // 2 - initial_sum` and if initial_sum is large, Python int. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a need to handle `M` maybe not equal to len(X) if input malformed; no.

Now, let's think about if there is a hidden issue with "N M" first line, but using data[0], data[1] works. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possibility that total=N and prefix condition passes but there is a stone at position x_i and count a_i, and pref before x_i >= x_i-1, but after adding, pref maybe > N? total=N so no. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "M <= N" but if M=2e5 and N=2e9, total=N, A_i can be large. Sorting pairs by X; if X list not sorted, sorting. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possibility that total=N and prefix condition passes but there is a gap before first position x1=1? If x1=1, okay. If x1=1 and A1 maybe 0? no. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a hidden issue with "cell i contains a stone" if cell i has a stone but cell i+1 is outside for i=N; operation only up to N-1. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possibility that total=N and prefix condition passes but answer formula final_sum - initial_sum maybe not minimal because you could choose to not move some stones and instead move others farther, but final sum fixed; total distance sum fixed. The number of operations equals total distance. If you move a stone farther, another must move less? But final cells distinct and total sum fixed, so total distance fixed. There is no way to reduce below fixed total distance because each operation increments potential by 1. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "minimum number of operations" if impossible due to prefix condition but maybe you can move stones right to create empty cells and then fill them with stones from left, but if no stone from left, impossible. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a hidden "N cells numbered from 1 to N in a row" and "Initially, M cells contain stones" maybe there are stones only in M cells, but if A_i sum=N, then some cells have multiple stones and some empty. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possibility that total=N and prefix condition passes but there is a stone at position x_i and count a_i, and a_i is so large that it fills cells beyond N? We showed fail if too large. But what if there are earlier stones that can fill some cells after x_i, reducing the need for stones at x_i to fill beyond N? Actually if a_i is large, stones at x_i cannot move left, so they must occupy cells >=x_i. If there are more stones at x_i than cells from x_i to N, impossible regardless of earlier stones because earlier stones cannot occupy cells <x_i? Wait earlier stones can occupy cells <x_i or >=x_i. But cells from x_i to N are N-x_i+1 cells. If A_i > N-x_i+1, even if all those cells are filled by stones at x_i, there are extra stones at x_i with no destination. Earlier stones cannot reduce the number of stones at x_i; they could occupy some cells >=x_i, making even fewer available. So impossible. Prefix condition catches as shown. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possibility that total=N and prefix condition passes but there is a stone at position x_i with count a_i and a_i > N - x_i +1 but pref before x_i >= x_i-1? Is that possible? Let's compute pref before = N - a_i - sum_after. If a_i > N-x_i+1, then N - a_i - sum_after < x_i -1 - sum_after <= x_i-1. So pref before < x_i-1. Thus fail. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a hidden issue with "minimum operations" if answer formula final_sum - initial_sum maybe not minimal because you could move a stone from i to i+1 and then later another stone from i+1 to i+2, but the first stone at i+1 might be moved further? That would be extra operations and final sum would exceed? No, if first stone moved further, its final position changes, but final sum fixed; another stone would have to end earlier, but stones can't move left, so maybe impossible. Minimal is direct. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possibility that total=N and prefix condition passes but there is a stone at position p assigned to final q, and another stone at position p' assigned to final q' with p<p' but q>q' (crossing). Could crossing reduce operations? Since final sum fixed, total distance = sum final positions - sum initial positions, independent of assignment. So crossing doesn't affect total operations. But crossing might be infeasible if q' < p'? Sorted matching avoids crossing and is feasible if condition. But any feasible assignment yields same total distance. So no issue. Our existence proof via greedy gives noncrossing assignment. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a hidden "minimum number of operations" if you can perform operation any number of times, but if a cell contains multiple stones, moving one stone from i to i+1 might be considered moving "a stone" but if there are multiple, you can choose which. Indistinguishable. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possibility that total=N and prefix condition passes but answer formula final_sum - initial_sum could be computed as `N * (N + 1) // 2 - initial_sum` and if initial_sum is large, Python int. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a need to handle `M` maybe not equal to len(X) if input malformed; no.

Now, let's think about if there is a hidden issue with "N M" first line, but using data[0], data[1] works. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possibility that total=N and prefix condition passes but there is a stone at position x_i and count a_i, and pref before x_i >= x_i-1, but after adding, pref maybe > N? total=N so no. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "M <= N" but if M=2e5 and N=2e9, total=N, A_i can be large. Sorting pairs by X; if X list not sorted, sorting. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possibility that total=N and prefix condition passes but there is a gap before first position x1=1? If x1=1, okay. If x1=1 and A1 maybe 0? no. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a hidden issue with "cell i contains a stone" if cell i has a stone but cell i+1 is outside for i=N; operation only up to N-1. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possibility that total=N and prefix condition passes but answer formula final_sum - initial_sum maybe not minimal because you could choose to not move some stones and instead move others farther, but final sum fixed; total distance sum fixed. The number of operations equals total distance. If you move a stone farther, another must move less? But final cells distinct and total sum fixed, so total distance fixed. There is no way to reduce below fixed total distance because each operation increments potential by 1. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "minimum number of operations" if impossible due to prefix condition but maybe you can move stones right to create empty cells and then fill them with stones from left, but if no stone from left, impossible. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a hidden "N cells numbered from 1 to N in a row" and "Initially, M cells contain stones" maybe there are stones only in M cells, but if A_i sum=N, then some cells have multiple stones and some empty. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possibility that total=N and prefix condition passes but there is a stone at position x_i and count a_i, and a_i is so large that it fills cells beyond N? We showed fail if too large. But what if there are earlier stones that can fill some cells after x_i, reducing the need for stones at x_i to fill beyond N? Actually if a_i is large, stones at x_i cannot move left, so they must occupy cells >=x_i. If there are more stones at x_i than cells from x_i to N, impossible regardless of earlier stones because earlier stones cannot occupy cells <x_i? Wait earlier stones can occupy cells <x_i or >=x_i. But cells from x_i to N are N-x_i+1 cells. If A_i > N-x_i+1, even if all those cells are filled by stones at x_i, there are extra stones at x_i with no destination. Earlier stones cannot reduce the number of stones at x_i; they could occupy some cells >=x_i, making even fewer available. So impossible. Prefix condition catches as shown. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possibility that total=N and prefix condition passes but there is a stone at position x_i with count a_i and a_i > N - x_i +1 but pref before x_i >= x_i-1? Is that possible? Let's compute pref before = N - a_i - sum_after. If a_i > N-x_i+1, then N - a_i - sum_after < x_i -1 - sum_after <= x_i-1. So pref before < x_i-1. Thus fail. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a hidden issue with "minimum operations" if answer formula final_sum - initial_sum maybe not minimal because you could move a stone from i to i+1 and then later another stone from i+1 to i+2, but the first stone at i+1 might be moved further? That would be extra operations and final sum would exceed? No, if first stone moved further, its final position changes, but final sum fixed; another stone would have to end earlier, but stones can't move left, so maybe impossible. Minimal is direct. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possibility that total=N and prefix condition passes but there is a stone at position p assigned to final q, and another stone at position p' assigned to final q' with p<p' but q>q' (crossing). Could crossing reduce operations? Since final sum fixed, total distance = sum final positions - sum initial positions, independent of assignment. So crossing doesn't affect total operations. But crossing might be infeasible if q' < p'? Sorted matching avoids crossing and is feasible if condition. But any feasible assignment yields same total distance. So no issue. Our existence proof via greedy gives noncrossing assignment. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possibility that total=N and prefix condition passes but answer formula final_sum - initial_sum maybe not minimal because final sum fixed but if stones are indistinguishable, maybe you don't need to assign each initial stone to a distinct final cell? But final has N stones in N cells, so yes each cell one stone, total N stones, so each initial stone corresponds to one final cell. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a hidden issue with "cell i contains a stone" if cell i has a stone but cell i+1 is occupied by a stone that is supposed to stay, moving into it would make two stones, but later maybe move one out. No restriction. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a need to handle `M` maybe not equal to len(X) if input malformed; no.

Now, let's think about if there is a hidden issue with "N M" first line, but using data[0], data[1] works. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possibility that total=N and prefix condition passes but there is a stone at position x_i and count a_i, and pref before x_i >= x_i-1, but after adding, pref maybe > N? total=N so no. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "M <= N" but if M=2e5 and N=2e9, total=N, A_i can be large. Sorting pairs by X; if X list not sorted, sorting. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possibility that total=N and prefix condition passes but there is a gap before first position x1=1? If x1=1, okay. If x1=1 and A1 maybe 0? no. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a hidden issue with "cell i contains a stone" if cell i has a stone but cell i+1 is outside for i=N; operation only up to N-1. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possibility that total=N and prefix condition passes but answer formula final_sum - initial_sum maybe not minimal because you could choose to not move some stones and instead move others farther, but final sum fixed; total distance sum fixed. The number of operations equals total distance. If you move a stone farther, another must move less? But final cells distinct and total sum fixed, so total distance fixed. There is no way to reduce below fixed total distance because each operation increments potential by 1. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "minimum number of operations" if impossible due to prefix condition but maybe you can move stones right to create empty cells and then fill them with stones from left, but if no stone from left, impossible. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a hidden "N cells numbered from 1 to N in a row" and "Initially, M cells contain stones" maybe there are stones only in M cells, but if A_i sum=N, then some cells have multiple stones and some empty. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possibility that total=N and prefix condition passes but there is a stone at position x_i and count a_i, and a_i is so large that it fills cells beyond N? We showed fail if too large. But what if there are earlier stones that can fill some cells after x_i, reducing the need for stones at x_i to fill beyond N? Actually if a_i is large, stones at x_i cannot move left, so they must occupy cells >=x_i. If there are more stones at x_i than cells from x_i to N, impossible regardless of earlier stones because earlier stones cannot occupy cells <x_i? Wait earlier stones can occupy cells <x_i or >=x_i. But cells from x_i to N are N-x_i+1 cells. If A_i > N-x_i+1, even if all those cells are filled by stones at x_i, there are extra stones at x_i with no destination. Earlier stones cannot reduce the number of stones at x_i; they could occupy some cells >=x_i, making even fewer available. So impossible. Prefix condition catches as shown. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possibility that total=N and prefix condition passes but there is a stone at position x_i with count a_i and a_i > N - x_i +1 but pref before x_i >= x_i-1? Is that possible? Let's compute pref before = N - a_i - sum_after. If a_i > N-x_i+1, then N - a_i - sum_after < x_i -1 - sum_after <= x_i-1. So pref before < x_i-1. Thus fail. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a hidden issue with "minimum operations" if answer formula final_sum - initial_sum maybe not minimal because you could move a stone from i to i+1 and then later another stone from i+1 to i+2, but the first stone at i+1 might be moved further? That would be extra operations and final sum would exceed? No, if first stone moved further, its final position changes, but final sum fixed; another stone would have to end earlier, but stones can't move left, so maybe impossible. Minimal is direct. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possibility that total=N and prefix condition passes but there is a stone at position p assigned to final q, and another stone at position p' assigned to final q' with p<p' but q>q' (crossing). Could crossing reduce operations? Since final sum fixed, total distance = sum final positions - sum initial positions, independent of assignment. So crossing doesn't affect total operations. But crossing might be infeasible if q' < p'? Sorted matching avoids crossing and is feasible if condition. But any feasible assignment yields same total distance. So no issue. Our existence proof via greedy gives noncrossing assignment. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possibility that total=N and prefix condition passes but answer formula final_sum - initial_sum maybe not minimal because final sum fixed but if stones are indistinguishable, maybe you don't need to assign each initial stone to a distinct final cell? But final has N stones in N cells, so yes each cell one stone, total N stones, so each initial stone corresponds to one final cell. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a hidden issue with "cell i contains a stone" if cell i has a stone but cell i+1 is occupied by a stone that is supposed to stay, moving into it would make two stones, but later maybe move one out. No restriction. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possibility that total=N and prefix condition passes but there is a stone at position p and assigned final q, and q-p operations. If multiple stones at same p, moving them one by one along same path; total operations sum. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a hidden "minimum number of operations" if you can perform operation any number of times, but if a cell contains multiple stones, moving one stone from i to i+1 might be considered moving "a stone" but if there are multiple, you can choose which. Indistinguishable. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possibility that total=N and prefix condition passes but there is a gap before first position x1=1? If x1=1, okay. If x1=1 and A1 maybe 0? no. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a hidden issue with "cell i contains a stone" if cell i has a stone but cell i+1 is outside for i=N; operation only up to N-1. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possibility that total=N and prefix condition passes but answer formula final_sum - initial_sum maybe not minimal because you could choose to not move some stones and instead move others farther, but final sum fixed; total distance sum fixed. The number of operations equals total distance. If you move a stone farther, another must move less? But final cells distinct and total sum fixed, so total distance fixed. There is no way to reduce below fixed total distance because each operation increments potential by 1. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "minimum number of operations" if impossible due to prefix condition but maybe you can move stones right to create empty cells and then fill them with stones from left, but if no stone from left, impossible. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a hidden "N cells numbered from 1 to N in a row" and "Initially, M cells contain stones" maybe there are stones only in M cells, but if A_i sum=N, then some cells have multiple stones and some empty. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possibility that total=N and prefix condition passes but there is a stone at position x_i and count a_i, and a_i is so large that it fills cells beyond N? We showed fail if too large. But what if there are earlier stones that can fill some cells after x_i, reducing the need for stones at x_i to fill beyond N? Actually if a_i is large, stones at x_i cannot move left, so they must occupy cells >=x_i. If there are more stones at x_i than cells from x_i to N, impossible regardless of earlier stones because earlier stones cannot occupy cells <x_i? Wait earlier stones can occupy cells <x_i or >=x_i. But cells from x_i to N are N-x_i+1 cells. If A_i > N-x_i+1, even if all those cells are filled by stones at x_i, there are extra stones at x_i with no destination. Earlier stones cannot reduce the number of stones at x_i; they could occupy some cells >=x_i, making even fewer available. So impossible. Prefix condition catches as shown. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possibility that total=N and prefix condition passes but there is a stone at position x_i with count a_i and a_i > N - x_i +1 but pref before x_i >= x_i-1? Is that possible? Let's compute pref before = N - a_i - sum_after. If a_i > N-x_i+1, then N - a_i - sum_after < x_i -1 - sum_after <= x_i-1. So pref before < x_i-1. Thus fail. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a hidden issue with "minimum operations" if answer formula final_sum - initial_sum maybe not minimal because you could move a stone from i to i+1 and then later another stone from i+1 to i+2, but the first stone at i+1 might be moved further? That would be extra operations and final sum would exceed? No, if first stone moved further, its final position changes, but final sum fixed; another stone would have to end earlier, but stones can't move left, so maybe impossible. Minimal is direct. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possibility that total=N and prefix condition passes but there is a stone at position p assigned to final q, and another stone at position p' assigned to final q' with p<p' but q>q' (crossing). Could crossing reduce operations? Since final sum fixed, total distance = sum final positions - sum initial positions, independent of assignment. So crossing doesn't affect total operations. But crossing might be infeasible if q' < p'? Sorted matching avoids crossing and is feasible if condition. But any feasible assignment yields same total distance. So no issue. Our existence proof via greedy gives noncrossing assignment. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possibility that total=N and prefix condition passes but answer formula final_sum - initial_sum maybe not minimal because final sum fixed but if stones are indistinguishable, maybe you don't need to assign each initial stone to a distinct final cell? But final has N stones in N cells, so yes each cell one stone, total N stones, so each initial stone corresponds to one final cell. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a hidden issue with "cell i contains a stone" if cell i has a stone but cell i+1 is occupied by a stone that is supposed to stay, moving into it would make two stones, but later maybe move one out. No restriction. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possibility that total=N and prefix condition passes but there is a stone at position p and assigned final q, and q-p operations. If multiple stones at same p, moving them one by one along same path; total operations sum. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a hidden "minimum number of operations" if you can perform operation any number of times, but if a cell contains multiple stones, moving one stone from i to i+1 might be considered moving "a stone" but if there are multiple, you can choose which. Indistinguishable. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possibility that total=N and prefix condition passes but answer formula final_sum - initial_sum could be computed as `N * (N + 1) // 2 - initial_sum` and if initial_sum is large, Python int. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a need to handle `M` maybe not equal to len(X) if input malformed; no.

Now, let's think about if there is a hidden issue with "N M" first line, but using data[0], data[1] works. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possibility that total=N and prefix condition passes but answer formula final_sum - initial_sum maybe not minimal because you could choose to not move some stones and instead move others farther, but final sum fixed; total distance sum fixed. The number of operations equals total distance. If you move a stone farther, another must move less? But final cells distinct and total sum fixed, so total distance fixed. There is no way to reduce below fixed total distance because each operation increments potential by 1. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "minimum number of operations" if impossible due to prefix condition but maybe you can move stones right to create empty cells and then fill them with stones from left, but if no stone from left, impossible. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a hidden "N cells numbered from 1 to N in a row" and "Initially, M cells contain stones" maybe there are stones only in M cells, but if A_i sum=N, then some cells have multiple stones and some empty. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possibility that total=N and prefix condition passes but there is a stone at position x_i and count a_i, and a_i is so large that it fills cells beyond N? We showed fail if too large. But what if there are earlier stones that can fill some cells after x_i, reducing the need for stones at x_i to fill beyond N? Actually if a_i is large, stones at x_i cannot move left, so they must occupy cells >=x_i. If there are more stones at x_i than cells from x_i to N, impossible regardless of earlier stones because earlier stones cannot occupy cells <x_i? Wait earlier stones can occupy cells <x_i or >=x_i. But cells from x_i to N are N-x_i+1 cells. If A_i > N-x_i+1, even if all those cells are filled by stones at x_i, there are extra stones at x_i with no destination. Earlier stones cannot reduce the number of stones at x_i; they could occupy some cells >=x_i, making even fewer available. So impossible. Prefix condition catches as shown. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possibility that total=N and prefix condition passes but there is a stone at position x_i with count a_i and a_i > N - x_i +1 but pref before x_i >= x_i-1? Is that possible? Let's compute pref before = N - a_i - sum_after. If a_i > N-x_i+1, then N - a_i - sum_after < x_i -1 - sum_after <= x_i-1. So pref before < x_i-1. Thus fail. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a hidden issue with "minimum operations" if answer formula final_sum - initial_sum maybe not minimal because you could move a stone from i to i+1 and then later another stone from i+1 to i+2, but the first stone at i+1 might be moved further? That would be extra operations and final sum would exceed? No, if first stone moved further, its final position changes, but final sum fixed; another stone would have to end earlier, but stones can't move left, so maybe impossible. Minimal is direct. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possibility that total=N and prefix condition passes but there is a stone at position p assigned to final q, and another stone at position p' assigned to final q' with p<p' but q>q' (crossing). Could crossing reduce operations? Since final sum fixed, total distance = sum final positions - sum initial positions, independent of assignment. So crossing doesn't affect total operations. But crossing might be infeasible if q' < p'? Sorted matching avoids crossing and is feasible if condition. But any feasible assignment yields same total distance. So no issue. Our existence proof via greedy gives noncrossing assignment. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possibility that total=N and prefix condition passes but answer formula final_sum - initial_sum maybe not minimal because final sum fixed but if stones are indistinguishable, maybe you don't need to assign each initial stone to a distinct final cell? But final has N stones in N cells, so yes each cell one stone, total N stones, so each initial stone corresponds to one final cell. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a hidden issue with "cell i contains a stone" if cell i has a stone but cell i+1 is occupied by a stone that is supposed to stay, moving into it would make two stones, but later maybe move one out. No restriction. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possibility that total=N and prefix condition passes but there is a stone at position p and assigned final q, and q-p operations. If multiple stones at same p, moving them one by one along same path; total operations sum. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a hidden "minimum number of operations" if you can perform operation any number of times, but if a cell contains multiple stones, moving one stone from i to i+1 might be considered moving "a stone" but if there are multiple, you can choose which. Indistinguishable. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possibility that total=N and prefix condition passes but answer formula final_sum - initial_sum could be computed as `N * (N + 1) // 2 - initial_sum` and if initial_sum is large, Python int. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a need to handle `M` maybe not equal to len(X) if input malformed; no.

Now, let's think about if there is a hidden issue with "N M" first line, but using data[0], data[1] works. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possibility that total=N and prefix condition passes but there is a stone at position x_i and count a_i, and pref before x_i >= x_i-1, but after adding, pref maybe > N? total=N so no. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "M <= N" but if M=2e5 and N=2e9, total=N, A_i can be large. Sorting pairs by X; if X list not sorted, sorting. Good.
